# Test run transcripts: adopt-casita-store-backend (2026-09-30)

Owner of this file: `mantle-store-adr`. It records runs that fixture owners
send, verbatim. A task is checked only when every fixture it lists has a
passing run recorded here. T3.4 is checked from Run 14 and T2.15 from Runs 26
and 29; no other task was checked from this file.

## Run 1: Casita GC unit filter, failed to compile

- Reported by: `mantle-casita-gc` (GC owner). Not rerun by the recorder.
- Command, as reported (mantle repository root):
  `nix develop -c cargo test -p crunch-store --lib gc::tests::casita_ -- --test-threads=1`
- Exit code: 101.
- Revision at run time: not reported by the runner. Revision at recording
  time: `7ec5177718a6950297e04eb4eb957a10b02e23ce` on `main...origin/main`, with 52 modified and 80
  untracked paths, so the tree was dirty and the run-time bytes are not
  pinned by this record.
- Fixtures run: none. `casita v0.1.0` at
  `90404fcb1cfb3d83f2233715448dfefe913f5fd1` failed with `E0599` at
  `crates/casita/src/nar.rs:88` (`hash.finalize().as_bytes()` on a
  `GenericArray`) from the unpatched git checkout under
  `~/.cargo/git/checkouts/`, before `crunch-store` compiled.
- Coverage: no Casita GC fixture ran, so T3.1 through T3.6 stay open. The run
  confirms that the pinned revision does not compile unmodified in Mantle's
  graph, which is the reason for the tracked patch in T4.6
  (`r[mantle.casita_store_backend.pinned_patch]`). T4.6 stays open.
- Not claimed: any passing test, patch application, adapter behavior, or GC
  safety.

## Run 2: Casita admission test before the `pathinfo.json` cutover, failed

- Reported by: `mantle-store-architecture` (adapter owner). Not rerun by the
  recorder.

  nothing.

  exercise Casita.
- Coverage, checked against the test source by the recorder: a two-path batch
  publishes both verified paths; 1,024 new roots import against a repository
  whose root-change limit is 1,024, and a reopened handle reads every path
  with the expected NAR hash; 1,025 new roots against a limit of 1,024 fail
  with `casita-batch-limit` naming the 1,025th path, and a reopened handle
  finds none of them; a conflicting existing root fails the batch naming that
  path, leaves the existing value, and publishes the other path nowhere; a bad
  NAR on the second path fails the batch naming it and publishes neither
  path; `trust_unsigned` fails before the archive is read; an unsigned record
  under a signed import fails. The tests do not assert that the 1,024 roots
  publish in one Casita revision, that the 1,025-root batch opens no mutation
  session, or that staged envelopes of a failed batch are reclaimable.

no task has every listed fixture passing.

  archive and Nario library APIs before reading input. No run covers the CLI
  rejections of `--trust-unsigned`, `--nario-trust-unsigned`, `--base-store`,
  or Rust unit cache use before any state access with the state directory
  unchanged.
- T3.5 stays open. Run 3 covers missing policy, same-name different-bytes

  signer that the import does not name, `store pin`, verification in a fresh
  OS process, or byte-identical source state directory and destination policy
  file.
- All other tasks stay open.

  - Newly retained candidate: covered on output roots by the same test. After
    the pin, the old `casita` plan fails with `gc-plan-stale` (the `snix` plan
    with `stale-gc-plan`), no Casita fence is written, and all three output
    roots remain.
  - Busy collector: covered end to end with castore roots
    (`casita_gc_busy_collector_keeps_reclaim_incomplete_until_recovery`):
    with the collector lock held, execution reports `reclaim-incomplete`,
    leaves the fence pending, and recovery then clears it.
  - Interruption before and after root removal: covered end to end only with
    castore roots (`casita_recovery_before_first_root_removal_preserves_candidate`,
    `casita_recovery_replays_real_partial_root_removal_without_new_deletions`).
    For output roots, only helper-level tests exist: fence journal parsing,
    export cleanup that keeps retained exports, and `casita-root-conflict` on
    a changed target.
  - Stale candidate target: not covered.
    `casita_plan_identity_binds_exact_root_targets` shows that the plan
    identity changes with a root target, but no test repoints a published
    envelope after planning and executes the old plan.
  - Clean execution on output roots: not covered. The only executed plan
    (`casita_gc_rejects_newly_retained_root_then_collects_only_unretained`)
    collects castore roots, which count only once `rust-unit-cache` is
    declared. It does assert physical collection: the dead castore payload
    opens through `repository.open_payload` before collection and not after,
    and the kept root still rehydrates.
  - `casita_gc_fence_pending_rejects_dangling_orphan_journal` shows the
    fence check failing closed on a dangling journal symlink (supports T3.2).

## Run 10: Casita CLI tests, failed a whole-directory byte comparison after a stale GC plan

- Reported by: CLI owner (`bg_728`). Not rerun by the recorder. The output is
  artifact://4203; its wall time and exit code come from the async result of
  `bg_728` in the CLI owner's session log.
- Git identity: captured at 17:41:20 UTC, 9 seconds before the command
  (artifact://4202), byte-identical to artifact://4196 (Run 9).
- Command (mantle repository root), started 17:41:29 UTC:
  `nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/brittonr/.cargo-target cargo test --locked --offline --config .cargo/vendor-config.toml -p mantle --test store_archive_cli casita_ -- --nocapture`
- Exit code: 101.
- Fixtures run: 3 run, 2 passed, 1 failed, 6 filtered out.
  `casita_rejects_unsigned_archive_before_creating_state` and
  `casita_rejects_base_store_and_nario_unsigned_before_state_access` passed.
  `snix_to_casita_cli_migration_pins_root_without_modifying_source` failed at
  `tests/store_archive_cli.rs:415:5`.
- What failed: at this revision line 415 was
  `assert_eq!(state_files(&target_state), before_stale_execution);`. It
  compared every file of the Casita state directory before and after a
  `store gc --execute` that used a stale plan. That command failed with
  `gc-plan-stale`, as the test required (the assertion before line 415 passed),
  but Casita files changed. The recorder compared the two maps printed on lines
  220 and 221. The left map (after the command) has 28 files and the right map
  (before) has 27:
  - only after the command:
    `casita/casita.sqlite.online-pins.reader-2c1e432044372a1bbc77cc09324d2c372c27d3fe468a2c4455448d89f27b3671.lock`,
    0 bytes;
  - changed content, same size: `casita/casita.sqlite.online-pins` (2,097,152
    bytes) and `casita/casita.sqlite.online-pins.readers` (158 bytes);
  - every other file is byte-identical, including `gc-roots.json`,
    `ca_mappings.json`, and the rest of `casita/`.
- Finding: Casita keeps its own reader-pin bookkeeping under
  `<state-dir>/casita`, and a command that opens the repository can change it
  even when Mantle mutates nothing. A byte comparison of the Casita directory
  is therefore not a "no mutation" check for such a command. The CLI owner then
  narrowed the check to the `gc-roots.json` bytes and the absence of
  `casita-gc-fence.json` and `casita-gc-fence.progress` (Run 11). ADR 0082
  records the constraint.
- Coverage: none.

## Run 11: Casita CLI tests, passed

- Reported by: CLI owner. The message gave the command, HEAD, exit code, test
  lines, and `test result:` line, and named `bg_729` for the full output. Not
  rerun by the recorder. The output below is the async result of `bg_729` in
  the CLI owner's session log.
- Git identity: captured at 17:46:12 UTC, 8 seconds before the command
  (artifact://4208), byte-identical to artifact://4196 (Run 9). Status lines do
  not pin content: the owner edited `tests/store_archive_cli.rs` at 17:46:00
  UTC, between the Run 10 and Run 11 captures.
- Command: as Run 10, started 17:46:21 UTC.
- Exit code: 0, as reported; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 3 passed, 6 filtered out.
- Test source: the recorder reconstructed what ran from the owner's reads of
  `tests/store_archive_cli.rs` after the run and before the owner's next edit
  to it (18:01:40 UTC). The migration test then did the following:
  1. exported a signed output under `snix` and recorded the source state bytes;
  2. wrote the signer's key into the destination `casita-trusted-public-keys`
     and imported with `--trusted-public-keys` under `casita`;
  3. checked `store info` JSON (`backend` is `casita`, `rust_unit_cache` false,
     `unsigned_admission` false, `max_root_changes` 1024) and the same three
     fields in human output;
  4. pinned the path, and `store roots` listed it;
  5. ran `store usage`, `store gc`, and `store gc --execute --plan-id` with the
     path pinned: usage and plan agreed, execution completed, and there were 0
     candidates;
  6. wrote `{"plan_id":"interrupted-plan","entries":[]}` to
     `casita-gc-fence.json`, and a guarded `store usage` removed it;
  7. with Rust unit cache retention state present, `store usage` and `store gc`
     failed with `casita-rust-cache-unsupported`, and the whole state directory
     stayed byte-identical;
  8. removed that state; a `casita` re-export equaled the `snix` archive byte
     for byte;
  9. unpinned, planned (1 candidate), pinned again, and executed the old plan:
     `gc-plan-stale`, `gc-roots.json` unchanged, and no `casita-gc-fence.json`
     or `casita-gc-fence.progress`; `store info` succeeded and another export
     equaled the original archive;
  10. unpinned and planned and executed again: 1 candidate, execution complete,
      and `store list` no longer showed the path;
  11. found the `snix` source state directory byte-identical to step 1.
  - At this revision the test ran no `store verify`, compared no signatures,
    and checked no policy-file bytes. The owner added those after the run, with
    a fourth `casita_` test for import negatives. None of the additions has
    run.
- Coverage, all partial; no task is checked:
  - T2.15: the `--trust-unsigned` import, `--nario-trust-unsigned` foreign
    import, and `--base-store` rejections pass without creating the state or
    store directory. Missing: a Rust unit cache command under `casita`, such as
    `rust-cache serve` or `rust-plan`. Step 7 is T3.6's negative, not a Rust
    unit cache command.
  - T3.6: the negative passes for `store gc` planning (step 7). The positive is
    partial: step 10 removes the only output, so no retained output stays
    beside it to verify afterward, as the scenario requires.
  - T3.3: newly retained candidate through the CLI (step 9), and
    plan-then-execute on one output root without a retained output beside it
    (step 10).
  - T3.2 and T3.7: a guarded `store usage` clears an empty fence file (step 6).
    No execution was interrupted after a root removal.
  - T3.5: the positive steps through `store pin`. Missing: fresh-process
    `store verify` with original signatures only, the policy-file byte check,
    and every negative.
  - T4.2: a `casita` re-export equals the `snix` export of the same closure
    (step 8). Missing: closures admitted by builds under both backends, and
    offline source preflight.
  - Casita T2.1 and selection T2.6: `casita` profile output only (step 3).
    Missing: `overlay-composition` in the output and the `snix` profile.

## Run 14: Casita adapter test, passed

- Reported by: adapter owner. Not rerun by the recorder. The output below is
  the async result of `bg_737` in the owner's session log.
- Git identity: none immediately before; see Run 12. The owner's last edits to
  `casita.rs` before the command were at 18:02:30, 18:02:37, 18:02:52, and
  18:04:45 UTC. The recorder read the test after the run. The file had not
  changed since 18:04:45: the owner's edit tool and the recorder's read report
  the same file snapshot.
- Command: as Run 13, started 18:04:50 UTC.
- Exit code: 0, as reported; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 1 passed, 379 filtered out:
  `casita::tests::signed_roots_survive_export_removal_and_reopen_with_conflicts_and_guard`.
  The test runs its own binary twice more as child OS processes (`--exact`).
  The children must print `casita-child-verified` and
  `casita-child-missing-verified`.
- What it asserts (`crates/crunch-store/src/casita.rs:729-1021`), with a
  destination policy file that holds fixture key 1:
  1. A content-addressed output `one`, signed by key 1, is admitted through
     `PathInfoService::put`. Its castore node is also published as a
     `mantle/castore/` root. The export is written, then deleted.
  2. A second handle's `put` of `one` with an extra signature fails with
     `casita-root-conflict`, twice.
  3. In the same process, a new handle's `ActionResultPort::probe_outputs`
     returns `one`, with `reused_nar_bytes` equal to its NAR size.
  4. `put_batch_atomic([two, three])` and `put_batch_atomic([one, four])`
     succeed.
  5. Child process 1, with the export gone: lookup returns `one`, its content
     is complete, the measured NAR facts equal the PathInfo, and
     `probe_outputs` returns `one`.
  6. A reopened handle rehydrates the castore root. `one` to `four` read back
     equal, `inspect_final_nar_repair` reports `Current`, and the export is
     restored with the original bytes.
  7. Without a guard, `garbage_collect(None)` and
     `garbage_collect(Some("one"))` fail with `casita-gc-guard-required`. A
     guard for another directory fails with `gc-plan-stale`. The right guard
     plans.
  8. Key 2 is added to the policy file. `store_sign` with key 2 appends a
     signature, and a lookup shows it.
  9. With a hand-written `casita-gc-fence.json` (`{}`), lookup, `put`, listing,
     export, castore rehydration, and root registration each fail with
     `gc-recovery-required`. After the file is removed, pinning `one` succeeds.
  10. A second client (`LocalRepository::local` on the same directory) reads
      `RootRetention::Permanent` for the four output roots and the castore
      root. `DiskPressurePolicy::new(1)` with `DiskUsage::new(100, 0)` returns
      `Collected`. Afterward all five roots are present and `Permanent`.
  11. The second client repoints `one` to envelopes with changed `content`
      (`casita-envelope-invalid`), with `nar_size + 1` in `pathinfo.json`
      (`casita-signer-untrusted`), with a trailing newline in `pathinfo.json`
      (`casita-envelope-invalid: PathInfo is not canonical JSON`), and with an
      oversized `pathinfo.json` (`casita-envelope-invalid: PathInfo size`).
      Each is checked by a lookup in the parent process.
  12. The second client removes the pinned root `one` with
      `RootChange::Remove`. Lookup and guarded GC planning fail with
      `casita-root-missing`, and the export and `two` are unchanged. In child
      process 2, lookup, `store_verify`, and guarded GC planning fail with
      `casita-root-missing`, the export bytes are unchanged, `two` resolves,
      and a never-admitted path is a plain miss (`None`).
- Coverage:
  - T3.4: every listed fixture passes. Step 10 is the real pressure-triggered
    collection that returns `Collected` with every root published and
    `Permanent` before and after. Step 12 is the retained root removed by
    another client, which reports `casita-root-missing` on verify and blocks GC
    planning, in the parent and in a fresh process. T3.4 is checked from this
    run.
  - T2.16: partial. Step 5 is a fresh-process `ActionResultPort` probe after
    the export was deleted. A `mantle/castore/` root for the same node existed
    (step 1), so the run does not show that rehydration needs no castore root.
    The action record is written by the test, not by a build.
  - T2.13: partial. A content-addressed output round-trips in a fresh process
    (step 5, with step 6 comparing the full PathInfo). The negatives in step 11
    run in the parent process, not a fresh one. Missing: an output without a
    content address, the double-encoding check, a NAR mismatch under a valid
    signature, a root-name digest mismatch, and a scratch copy after a failed
    publication.
  - T2.9: partial (steps 2 and 4). Missing: the staging-to-commit race,
    idempotent success without a write, and a stop after staging.
  - T2.10: partial (step 8, same process). Missing: fresh-process verification,
    the old-envelope check, repoint races during `store sign` and
    `store repair-final-nar`, and a mismatched repair.
  - T3.7: partial (steps 7 and 9). Missing: assertions that nothing changed, a
    real execution interrupted after root removal, a fresh process, and guarded
    recovery.
  - T4.3: none. No build runs, so the run shows no cache hit.

  artifact://4293; its wall time comes from the async result of `bg_747` in the
  GC owner's session log.
- Command (mantle repository root), started 18:16:41 UTC. It prints the git
  identity itself, immediately before the test:
  `git rev-parse HEAD && git status --short --branch && env CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/brittonr/.cargo-target nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command cargo test -p crunch-store --lib gc::tests::casita_ --locked --offline -- --test-threads=1 --nocapture`
- Exit code: 0, as reported; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Revision: HEAD `7ec5177718a6950297e04eb4eb957a10b02e23ce`; the status lines
  at the top are identical to Run 17's (dirty tree). The GC owner's last edit
  to `crates/crunch-store/src/gc.rs` before the run was at 18:15:35 UTC. The
  recorder read the tests after the run, and the file had not changed since
  that edit (same file snapshot).
- Fixtures run: 15 passed, 369 filtered out. They are the 12 tests of Run 7
  plus three new ones:
  - `casita_gc_repointed_verified_output_rejects_old_plan_before_fencing`: a
    signed output root is repointed, after planning, to another verified
    envelope (same signed facts, different deriver). The old plan fails with
    `gc-plan-stale`, no fence is pending, and the root keeps its new target.
  - `casita_output_fence_recovery_preserves_unremoved_and_cleans_only_removed_metadata`:
    two signed outputs, each with an export, an action-result record and index
    marker, and a CA mapping, and a plan with both as candidates. Phase 1
    persists the fence and reopens the store: a lookup fails with
    `gc-recovery-required`, and guarded recovery
    (`recover_casita_gc_under_guard`) leaves both roots, exports, records,
    markers, and mappings intact. Phase 2 persists the fence again, removes the
    first root with `remove_root_if_matches` without an outcome record, and
    reopens the store. Guarded recovery then removes only the first output's
    export, record, index marker, and CA mapping and keeps the second. The
    recorder counts this as built crash state (fence written, one root removed,
    no outcome recorded), not a killed process.
  - `casita_fence_rejects_symlink_and_oversized_outcome_journal`: fence-journal
    hardening, not a listed fixture.
- Coverage:
  - T3.3: stale candidate target, newly retained candidate, interruption before
    and after root removal, busy collector, and equal decisions with `snix` now
    pass on output roots, except the busy collector, which runs on castore
    roots (collection is repository-wide). The Rust-retention payload clause
    applies only once `rust-unit-cache` is declared. One gap keeps T3.3 open: a
    clean plan-then-execute on output roots in which a retained output stays
    verifiable afterward. The only executed output-root plan (Run 11, step 10)
    removes the sole output, and the executed plans here collect castore roots.
  - T3.7: partial. Phase 1 shows `gc-recovery-required` for a lookup after a
    reopen in the same process, and guarded recovery leaves every unremoved
    fenced root published. Missing: the other operations, a fresh process,
    "nothing changed" assertions after an interruption after root removal, and
    recovery through `store gc`.

Command, verbatim from the owner's message:

```bash
nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh python3 -c 'import base64, hashlib, json, os, pathlib, subprocess, tempfile
BINARY = "/home/brittonr/.cargo-target/debug/mantle"
REPO = pathlib.Path("/home/brittonr/git/OnixResearch/mantle")
KEYPAIR = "archive-cli-1:cCta2MEsRNuYCgWYyeRXLyfoFpKhQJKn8gLMeXWAb7vIpRKKo/3JoxJ24OYa3DxT2JVV38KjK/1ywHWuMe2JEw=="
PUBLIC_KEY = "archive-cli-1:" + base64.b64encode(base64.b64decode(KEYPAIR.split(":", 1)[1])[32:]).decode()
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="mantle-casita-build-cache-") as scratch:
    root = pathlib.Path(scratch)
    payload = b"durable CLI fresh-process cache fixture\n"
    source = root / "source.txt"
    source.write_bytes(payload)
    sri = "sha256-" + base64.b64encode(hashlib.sha256(payload).digest()).decode()
    fixture = root / "fetch.ncl"
    fixture.write_text(f'"'"'let mantle = import "{REPO / "lib/lib.ncl"}" in\nmantle.fetchurl {{ url = "file://{source}", hash = "{sri}", name = "backend-cache-fixture" }}\n'"'"')
    observations = {}
    for backend in ("casita", "snix"):
        state, store = root / f"{backend}-state", root / f"{backend}-store"
        state.mkdir()
        store.mkdir()
        signer = state / "signing-key"
        signer.write_text(KEYPAIR + "\n")
        if backend == "casita":
            (state / "casita-trusted-public-keys").write_text(PUBLIC_KEY + "\n")
        def run(*action):
            argv = [BINARY, "--store-backend", backend, "--state-dir", str(state), "--store", str(store), *map(str, action)]
            outcome = subprocess.run(argv, cwd=REPO, capture_output=True, text=True, env=os.environ.copy(), timeout=90)
            if outcome.returncode:
                raise AssertionError(f"{backend} {action} exited {outcome.returncode}: stderr={outcome.stderr[:4000]} stdout={outcome.stdout[:4000]}")
            return outcome
        build_args = ("--json", "build", "--no-substitute", "--signing-key", str(signer), "--trusted-public-keys", PUBLIC_KEY, str(fixture))
        first = json.loads(run(*build_args).stdout)
        assert first["counts"]["built_total"] == 1 and first["outcomes"][0]["cached"] is False, (backend, first["counts"])
        output = pathlib.Path(first["outcomes"][0]["outputs"][0]["path"])
        assert output.parent == store and output.is_file(), (backend, output)
        assert output.read_bytes() == payload
        original_info = json.loads(run("--json", "store", "info", output.name).stdout)["paths"][0]
        assert original_info["signatures"] and all(signature.startswith("archive-cli-1:") for signature in original_info["signatures"])
        output.unlink()
        assert not output.exists(), (backend, "physical export was not removed")
        second = json.loads(run(*build_args).stdout)
        assert second["counts"]["cached_total"] == 1 and second["counts"]["built_total"] == 0 and second["outcomes"][0]["cached"] is True, (backend, second["counts"])
        restored = pathlib.Path(second["outcomes"][0]["outputs"][0]["path"])
        assert restored == output and restored.is_file() and restored.read_bytes() == payload, (backend, restored)
        restored_info = json.loads(run("--json", "store", "info", restored.name).stdout)["paths"][0]
        for field in ("store_path", "nar_sha256", "nar_size", "signatures"):
            assert restored_info[field] == original_info[field], (backend, field, original_info[field], restored_info[field])
        verification = run("store", "verify", "--trusted-public-keys", PUBLIC_KEY, restored.name).stdout.strip()
        assert "trusted_signatures=1/1" in verification, (backend, verification)
        observations[backend] = {"first_counts": first["counts"], "second_counts": second["counts"], "store_path": restored_info["store_path"], "nar_sha256": restored_info["nar_sha256"], "nar_size": restored_info["nar_size"], "verification": verification}
    for field in ("store_path", "nar_sha256", "nar_size"):
        assert observations["casita"][field] == observations["snix"][field], (field, observations)
    print(json.dumps({"status": "PASS", "backends": observations}, sort_keys=True))
'
```

## Run 23: Casita CLI archive tests, before the trust fix, passed

- Reported by: CLI owner (`bg_763`). Not rerun by the recorder. The output
  below is the async result of `bg_763` in the owner's session log. The owner
  marked this run as pre-fix: the adapter still trusts the local signing key
  beside a present policy file.
- Git identity: captured at 18:43:55 UTC, 13 seconds before the command
  (artifact://4420, byte-identical to artifact://4348 in Run 21).
- Test source: `tests/store_archive_cli.rs`. The owner's last edit before the
  run was at 18:40:00 UTC and added `mantle_state_files`, which compares
  Mantle-owned files and skips `casita/` and `store-mutation.lock`. The
  recorder read the file after the run, and it had not changed since that edit
  (same file snapshot).
- Command (mantle repository root), started 18:44:08 UTC:
  `nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/brittonr/.cargo-target cargo test --locked --offline --config .cargo/vendor-config.toml -p mantle --test store_archive_cli casita_ -- --nocapture`
- Exit code: 0; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 4 passed, 6 filtered out. Three are the tests of Run 11 as they
  stand now. The fourth,
  `casita_archive_import_rejects_unprovisioned_keys_and_unnamed_signer_without_mutation`,
  imports a signed `snix` archive into a new `casita` state directory in four
  cases: no policy file (`casita-trust-policy-missing`); a policy that lacks
  the import key (`casita-import-key-unauthorized`); a policy key with the
  import key's name but different bytes (`casita-import-key-unauthorized`); and
  an archive whose signer the import does not name (`untrusted-signature`). In
  each case the Mantle-owned state files and the store directory keep their
  bytes, the policy file is unchanged or still absent, and `store list` shows
  no path. The migration test now also runs `store verify` in a new process
  (`trusted_signatures=1/1`), compares the imported signatures with the
  source's, and checks the destination policy file's bytes at the end.
- Coverage:
  - T3.5: every listed fixture now has a passing run. The positive migration is
    here; the named-key negatives are here; the unsigned record in a signed
    import is covered by the library tests in Runs 3 and 4; the
    `--trust-unsigned` and `--nario-trust-unsigned` rejections are here and in
    Run 11. One requirement is still missing, so T3.5 stays open: "every
    fixture asserts a byte-identical source state directory". The import
    negatives here and in Run 3 do not compare the source state directory.
  - T2.15: unchanged from Run 11. The flag rejections pass; a Rust unit cache
    command is still missing.
  - T2.14: not covered. These are import-authorization tests, not local-signer
    tests.

## Run 24: local-signer trust test, failed before the trust fix

- Reported by: CLI owner (`bg_765`) as a failing-before run for the trust fix.
  Not rerun by the recorder. The output below is the async result of `bg_765`
  in the owner's session log.
- Git identity: captured at 18:44:57 UTC, 5 seconds before the command
  (artifact://4430, byte-identical to artifact://4348 in Run 21).
- Test source: `tests/integration_build.rs:1083-1166`, unchanged since the
  owner's 18:41:26 UTC edit (same file snapshot as Run 22).
- Command (mantle repository root), started 18:45:02 UTC:
  `nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/brittonr/.cargo-target cargo test --locked --offline --config .cargo/vendor-config.toml -p mantle --test integration_build casita_explicit_trust_policy_revokes_local_signer_across_fresh_processes -- --nocapture`
- Exit code: 101.
- Fixtures run: 1 failed, 13 filtered out:
  `casita_explicit_trust_policy_revokes_local_signer_across_fresh_processes`.
  The test writes a policy file that lists only another key and builds with the
  local signing key. It expects `casita-signer-untrusted`, but the build
  succeeded, so the test panicked at line 1126 with "unlisted local signer
  built an output". Its later steps never ran: listing the local key and
  building, removing the key and expecting `store info` to fail with
  `casita-signer-untrusted`, and restoring the key.
- Coverage: none as a pass. It shows that the adapter at this revision still
  trusts the local signing key beside a policy file that does not list it,
  which is the old rule. The ADR and spec now require the new rule.

  started 18:49:48 UTC after an identity capture at 18:49:43 UTC
  (artifact://4450). Exit 0, 11 passed, 376 filtered out.

  (artifact://4457, byte-identical to artifact://4348 in Run 21).
- Test source: `tests/store_archive_cli.rs`, unchanged since the owner's
  18:47:09 UTC edit (same file snapshot). That edit takes a snapshot of the
  source state directory right after the `snix` export (line 234). Each of the
  four rejected-import cases now asserts that the source state directory is
  byte-identical (line 285).
- Command (mantle repository root), started 18:51:29 UTC:
  `nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/brittonr/.cargo-target cargo test --locked --offline --config .cargo/vendor-config.toml -p mantle --test store_archive_cli casita_ -- --nocapture`
- Exit code: 0; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 4 passed, 6 filtered out; the same tests as Run 23, plus the
  source-state assertion.
- Coverage: T3.5 stays open for one fixture. The unsigned record in a signed
  import is covered only by
  `archive::tests::casita_archive_rejects_unsigned_record_under_signed_import`
  (Run 3) and
  `nario::tests::nario_casita_rejects_unsigned_record_under_signed_import` (Run

  published. They do not assert that the source state directory and the
  destination policy file keep their bytes, which T3.5 requires of every
  fixture. Every other T3.5 fixture now passes with all its assertions (Runs
  11, 23, and 26). The `--trust-unsigned` and `--nario-trust-unsigned` fixtures
  create no state directory, so no source or policy comparison applies to them.

## Run 27: `store_gc_cli` tests, failed on the legacy-migration test

- Reported by: CLI owner, who called it the failing-before run of the
  legacy-migration test ("previous test failed-before"). Not rerun by the
  recorder. The output below is the async result of `bg_766` in the owner's
  session log. This run happened before Runs 25 and 26.
- Git identity: captured at 18:45:30 UTC, 5 seconds before the command
  (artifact://4434, byte-identical to artifact://4348 in Run 21).
- Command (mantle repository root), started 18:45:35 UTC:
  `nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/brittonr/.cargo-target cargo test --locked --offline --config .cargo/vendor-config.toml -p mantle --test store_gc_cli -- --nocapture`
- Exit code: 101.
- Fixtures run: 11 run, 10 passed, 1 failed, 0 filtered out.
  `store_roots_migrates_legacy_records_without_inventing_ownership` failed.
  `store roots --migrate` on a state directory that held legacy root records
  but no identity record exited 3 with
  `store-backend-mismatch: state directory ... has content but no backend identity`.
- Finding: the open decision (`crates/crunch-store/src/overlay.rs:193-213`)
  rejects any non-empty state directory without an identity record, for both
  backends. The only exceptions are `store-mutation.lock`, `signing-key`,
  `casita-trusted-public-keys`, and `overlay-trusted-public-keys`. At HEAD,
  `ensure_store_identity` wrote a v1 record into such a directory instead. The
  owner changed the test to record a `snix` identity first (Run 29). The parent
  then chose an asymmetric rule, now in the selection spec and ADR 0082. Under
  `snix`, identity-less state without a `casita` marker is claimed as legacy
  Snix state. An identity-less `casita` marker fails under either backend, and
  `casita` rejects identity-less content outside the allowlist. The code for
  that rule has since landed in `preflight_store_identity`, and its fixtures
  pass in selection Run 9 (selection Runs 6 to 8 are the earlier attempts).
- Coverage: none.

  - `casita_rust_cache_daemon_fails_before_loading_policy_or_creating_state`
    (`store_gc_cli.rs:407-424`):
    `--store-backend casita rust-cache serve --policy <missing> --receipt-dir <dir>`
    fails with `casita-rust-cache-unsupported`. The state, store, and receipt
    directories are not created.
  - `casita_rust_plan_cache_modes_fail_before_cargo_capture` (`:426-441`):
    `rust-plan --execute-first-supported-unit` with `--local-rust-cache` and
    with `--shared-rust-cache` under `casita` fails with
    `casita-rust-cache-unsupported`. The state and store directories are not
    created.
  - `store_info_reports_base_layer_without_mutating_base` (`:288-346`):
    `store info` for `snix` reports all four optional capabilities as true and
    `max_root_changes` as null in JSON, and
    `max-root-changes: unbounded-by-backend` in human output.
  - `selected_backend_is_recorded_and_mismatch_rejects_without_mutation`
    (`:369-390`): `--store-backend casita` records `casita`. A later
    `--store-backend snix` fails with `store-backend-mismatch`, the identity
    file keeps its bytes, and the entry count of the state directory does not
    change.
  - `unknown_backend_is_rejected_before_creating_state` (`:392-405`):
    `--store-backend tape` fails with `store-backend-unknown` and creates
    neither directory.

    state directory records `snix`.

    `--nario-trust-unsigned`, and `--base-store` rejections pass without
    creating the state directory (Runs 11, 23, 26), and the Rust unit cache
    commands `rust-cache serve` and `rust-plan` in both cache modes fail with
    `casita-rust-cache-unsupported` before any effect (this run). T2.15 is
    checked from Runs 26 and 29.

    `../../add-store-backend-selection/evidence/test-runs-2026-09-30.md`.

- Reported by: CLI owner. Not rerun by the recorder. The command is copied from
  the owner's message (3,532 bytes, SHA-256
  `92f7c84912ea6a993b65d4bb2ada718123fb869dcac1a056374ce160c36f31e3`). The
  recorder checked it against the command string the owner built in their eval
  kernel (18:44:37 UTC) and executed at 18:57:06 UTC. The output is
  artifact://4492; the wall time comes from artifact://4491.
- Git identity and binary digest: captured at 18:57:00 UTC, 6 seconds before
  the smoke, in one command:
  `git rev-parse HEAD && git status --short --branch && b3sum /home/brittonr/.cargo-target/debug/mantle`
  (artifact://4490, SHA-256
  `e7206cc3a1b7199cf6e3f1d519c7eed75a3335f7133cda55def02fd99e0d5331`, recorded
  below). HEAD `7ec5177718a6950297e04eb4eb957a10b02e23ce`, dirty tree. The
  binary's BLAKE3 is
  `185cede53de72e875c2a618a0dd43046458eeff9b5e51701b767c62e1fbbbebb`. It is the
  binary that a `--config .cargo/vendor-config.toml` build left in the shared
  target directory, which ties the smoke to one executable but not to exact
  source bytes.
- Exit code: 0; the tool result has no non-zero exit footer.
- What the run shows. The JSON output records, for `casita` and `snix`:
  `built_total` 1 on the first build; `cached_total` 1 and `built_total` 0 on
  the second; store path `z2y3k7267f9kbpfyw9zw0d1adjcn6z62-hello`; NAR SHA-256
  `86b90edc64f470240656da9a80f655d3b57c19be6b9ba41e03e4596732e1dd01`; NAR size
  128; the same `cache.example.com-1` signature; and `trusted_signatures=1/1`
  from `store verify`. The build is the repository's `examples/hello.ncl`: a
  `/bin/sh` builder that writes `Hello, mantle!`, run in the local `bwrap`
  sandbox with `--no-substitute` and `--import-path lib`. The derivation is
  content-addressed: `mantle.Derivation` defaults `addressing_mode` to
  `'content-addressed` (`lib/derivation.ncl:90-92`), and the example does not
  override it. The script's JSON label `sandboxed-input-addressed` is therefore
  wrong. Before printing the JSON, the script asserts: the first build is not
  cached; only the physical output is deleted; the second build in a new
  process is cached and restores the same path with the same bytes; and the
  `store info` fields are equal before and after, and across the two backends.
  For `casita` the policy file lists the signing key.
- Coverage:
  - The spec scenario "Export deleted before the next build" passes under
    `casita` for one sandboxed content-addressed derivation. With Runs 21 and
    22, the scenario passes for a fixed-output fetch and a sandboxed build.
  - T4.3: the fresh-process fixture passes for both derivation kinds. T4.3
    stays open for the backend conformance rail.
  - Not shown: `ActionResultPort` (T2.16), GC, concurrency, measured network
    isolation, or builders beyond this one fixture.

Command, verbatim from the owner's message:

```bash
nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh python3 -c 'import base64, json, os, pathlib, subprocess, tempfile
BINARY = "/home/brittonr/.cargo-target/debug/mantle"
REPO = pathlib.Path("/home/brittonr/git/OnixResearch/mantle")
KEYPAIR = "cache.example.com-1:cCta2MEsRNuYCgWYyeRXLyfoFpKhQJKn8gLMeXWAb7vIpRKKo/3JoxJ24OYa3DxT2JVV38KjK/1ywHWuMe2JEw=="
PUBLIC_KEY = "cache.example.com-1:" + base64.b64encode(base64.b64decode(KEYPAIR.split(":", 1)[1])[32:]).decode()
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="mantle-casita-sandboxed-cache-") as scratch:
    root = pathlib.Path(scratch)
    observations = {}
    for backend in ("casita", "snix"):
        state, store = root / f"{backend}-state", root / f"{backend}-store"
        state.mkdir(); store.mkdir()
        signer = state / "signing-key"; signer.write_text(KEYPAIR + "\n")
        if backend == "casita":
            (state / "casita-trusted-public-keys").write_text(PUBLIC_KEY + "\n")
        def run(*action):
            argv = [BINARY, "--store-backend", backend, "--state-dir", str(state), "--store", str(store), *map(str, action)]
            out = subprocess.run(argv, cwd=REPO, capture_output=True, text=True, env=os.environ.copy(), timeout=90)
            if out.returncode: raise AssertionError(f"{backend} {action} exit {out.returncode}: stderr={out.stderr[:6000]} stdout={out.stdout[:6000]}")
            return out
        args = ("--json", "build", "--no-substitute", "--signing-key", signer, "--trusted-public-keys", PUBLIC_KEY, "--import-path", REPO / "lib", REPO / "examples/hello.ncl")
        first = json.loads(run(*args).stdout)
        assert first["counts"]["built_total"] == 1 and first["outcomes"][0]["cached"] is False, first
        path = pathlib.Path(first["outcomes"][0]["outputs"][0]["path"])
        assert path.parent == store and path.read_bytes() == b"Hello, mantle!\n", (backend, path)
        info = json.loads(run("--json", "store", "info", path.name).stdout)["paths"][0]
        assert info["signatures"] and all(s.startswith("cache.example.com-1:") for s in info["signatures"])
        path.unlink()
        assert not path.exists()
        second = json.loads(run(*args).stdout)
        assert second["counts"]["cached_total"] == 1 and second["counts"]["built_total"] == 0 and second["outcomes"][0]["cached"] is True, second
        restored = pathlib.Path(second["outcomes"][0]["outputs"][0]["path"])
        assert restored == path and restored.read_bytes() == b"Hello, mantle!\n", (backend, restored)
        new_info = json.loads(run("--json", "store", "info", path.name).stdout)["paths"][0]
        for field in ("store_path", "nar_sha256", "nar_size", "signatures"):
            assert new_info[field] == info[field], (backend, field)
        verified = run("store", "verify", "--trusted-public-keys", PUBLIC_KEY, path.name).stdout.strip()
        assert "trusted_signatures=1/1" in verified, verified
        observations[backend] = {"first_counts": first["counts"], "second_counts": second["counts"], "store_path": new_info["store_path"], "nar_sha256": new_info["nar_sha256"], "nar_size": new_info["nar_size"], "signatures": new_info["signatures"], "verified": verified}
    for field in ("store_path", "nar_sha256", "nar_size", "signatures"):
        assert observations["casita"][field] == observations["snix"][field], (field, observations)
    print(json.dumps({"status": "PASS", "scenario": "sandboxed-input-addressed", "backends": observations}, sort_keys=True))
'
```

## Run 31: fresh-process `mantle build` cache smoke with a sandboxed input-addressed build, passed

- Reported by: CLI owner, as the corrected follow-up to Run 30. Not rerun by
  the recorder. The command is copied from the owner's message (3,829 bytes,
  SHA-256 `65bf0b8d591c3646677f686201db7b3c964b13b0e045a26f75a64580e009b9cd`).
  The recorder rebuilt it from the owner's eval-kernel edits at 18:59:47 and
  19:00:06 UTC and got the same bytes. It ran as `bg_774` at 19:00:24 UTC. The
  output is artifact://4528; the wall time comes from the async result of
  `bg_774`.
- Git identity and binary digest: captured at 19:00:18 UTC, 6 seconds before
  the smoke (artifact://4525). It is byte-identical to artifact://4490 in Run
  30, so the HEAD, status, and binary BLAKE3
  (`185cede53de72e875c2a618a0dd43046458eeff9b5e51701b767c62e1fbbbebb`) are the
  same.
- Exit code: 0; the tool result has no non-zero exit footer.
- What the run shows. The script copies `examples/hello.ncl` into a temporary
  file and inserts `addressing_mode = 'input-addressed` after its `builder`
  line, so the derivation is input-addressed. It then runs the same steps as
  Run 30 for `casita` and `snix`. The JSON output records, for both backends:
  `built_total` 1 on the first build; `cached_total` 1 and `built_total` 0 on
  the second; store path `jq82if5iajpp94w5qzm0v8bspq0y6ax7-hello`, which
  differs from Run 30's content-addressed path; the same NAR SHA-256 as Run 30
  (`86b90edc64f470240656da9a80f655d3b57c19be6b9ba41e03e4596732e1dd01`) and NAR
  size 128; the same signature; and `trusted_signatures=1/1`. The script
  asserts the same conditions as in Run 30 before it prints.
- Coverage: the spec scenario "Export deleted before the next build" now passes
  under `casita` for a fixed-output fetch (Runs 21 and 22), a sandboxed
  content-addressed build (Run 30), and a sandboxed input-addressed build (this
  run), one fixture each. T4.3 stays open for the backend conformance rail. Not
  shown: `ActionResultPort` (T2.16), GC, concurrency, or measured network
  isolation.

Command, verbatim from the owner's message:

```bash
nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh python3 -c 'import base64, json, os, pathlib, subprocess, tempfile
BINARY = "/home/brittonr/.cargo-target/debug/mantle"
REPO = pathlib.Path("/home/brittonr/git/OnixResearch/mantle")
KEYPAIR = "cache.example.com-1:cCta2MEsRNuYCgWYyeRXLyfoFpKhQJKn8gLMeXWAb7vIpRKKo/3JoxJ24OYa3DxT2JVV38KjK/1ywHWuMe2JEw=="
PUBLIC_KEY = "cache.example.com-1:" + base64.b64encode(base64.b64decode(KEYPAIR.split(":", 1)[1])[32:]).decode()
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="mantle-casita-sandboxed-cache-") as scratch:
    root = pathlib.Path(scratch)
    fixture = root / "hello-input.ncl"
    template = (REPO / "examples/hello.ncl").read_text()
    assert template.count('"'"'builder = "/bin/sh",'"'"') == 1
    fixture.write_text(template.replace('"'"'builder = "/bin/sh",'"'"', '"'"'builder = "/bin/sh",\n  addressing_mode = \'"'"'input-addressed,'"'"'))
    observations = {}
    for backend in ("casita", "snix"):
        state, store = root / f"{backend}-state", root / f"{backend}-store"
        state.mkdir(); store.mkdir()
        signer = state / "signing-key"; signer.write_text(KEYPAIR + "\n")
        if backend == "casita":
            (state / "casita-trusted-public-keys").write_text(PUBLIC_KEY + "\n")
        def run(*action):
            argv = [BINARY, "--store-backend", backend, "--state-dir", str(state), "--store", str(store), *map(str, action)]
            out = subprocess.run(argv, cwd=REPO, capture_output=True, text=True, env=os.environ.copy(), timeout=90)
            if out.returncode: raise AssertionError(f"{backend} {action} exit {out.returncode}: stderr={out.stderr[:6000]} stdout={out.stdout[:6000]}")
            return out
        args = ("--json", "build", "--no-substitute", "--signing-key", signer, "--trusted-public-keys", PUBLIC_KEY, "--import-path", REPO / "lib", fixture)
        first = json.loads(run(*args).stdout)
        assert first["counts"]["built_total"] == 1 and first["outcomes"][0]["cached"] is False, first
        path = pathlib.Path(first["outcomes"][0]["outputs"][0]["path"])
        assert path.parent == store and path.read_bytes() == b"Hello, mantle!\n", (backend, path)
        info = json.loads(run("--json", "store", "info", path.name).stdout)["paths"][0]
        assert info["signatures"] and all(s.startswith("cache.example.com-1:") for s in info["signatures"])
        path.unlink()
        assert not path.exists()
        second = json.loads(run(*args).stdout)
        assert second["counts"]["cached_total"] == 1 and second["counts"]["built_total"] == 0 and second["outcomes"][0]["cached"] is True, second
        restored = pathlib.Path(second["outcomes"][0]["outputs"][0]["path"])
        assert restored == path and restored.read_bytes() == b"Hello, mantle!\n", (backend, restored)
        new_info = json.loads(run("--json", "store", "info", path.name).stdout)["paths"][0]
        for field in ("store_path", "nar_sha256", "nar_size", "signatures"):
            assert new_info[field] == info[field], (backend, field)
        verified = run("store", "verify", "--trusted-public-keys", PUBLIC_KEY, path.name).stdout.strip()
        assert "trusted_signatures=1/1" in verified, verified
        observations[backend] = {"first_counts": first["counts"], "second_counts": second["counts"], "store_path": new_info["store_path"], "nar_sha256": new_info["nar_sha256"], "nar_size": new_info["nar_size"], "signatures": new_info["signatures"], "verified": verified}
    for field in ("store_path", "nar_sha256", "nar_size", "signatures"):
        assert observations["casita"][field] == observations["snix"][field], (field, observations)
    print(json.dumps({"status": "PASS", "scenario": "sandboxed-input-addressed-explicit", "backends": observations}, sort_keys=True))
'
```

## Run 32: local-signer trust test after the adapter trust fix, failed on its stderr check

- Found by the recorder in the adapter owner's session log (`bg_776`). The
  owner reported only the later pass (Run 33). Not rerun by the recorder. The
  output is artifact://4557; its wall time and exit code come from the async
  result of `bg_776`.
- Source: the owner's trust fix in `crates/crunch-store/src/casita.rs`
  (19:00:18 UTC) makes a present `casita-trusted-public-keys` file the whole
  trust set; the local verifying key is added only when the file is absent. The
  fix also validates the policy file at store open. The owner's last edit to
  `casita.rs` before this run was at 19:02:58 UTC. The test was still the Run
  24 version of `tests/integration_build.rs` (same file snapshot).
- Command (mantle repository root), started 19:03:05 UTC. It prints the git
  identity itself, right before the test:
  `git rev-parse HEAD && git status --short --branch && nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/brittonr/.cargo-target cargo test --locked --offline --config .cargo/vendor-config.toml -p mantle --test integration_build casita_explicit_trust_policy_revokes_local_signer_across_fresh_processes -- --nocapture`
- Exit code: 101.
- Fixtures run: 1 failed, 13 filtered out. The unlisted-signer build now fails,
  so line 1126 passed. The test then panicked at line 1127, which looked for
  `casita-signer-untrusted` on stderr and found an empty message. With
  `--json`, the blocker is reported in the JSON report on stdout instead. The
  CLI owner changed lines 1127-1134 at 19:06:29 UTC to read `failed[0].message`
  from that report.
- Coverage: none.

## Run 33: local-signer trust test after the adapter trust fix, passed

- Reported by: adapter owner (`bg_778`). Not rerun by the recorder. The output
  is artifact://4570; its wall time comes from the async result of `bg_778`.
- Source: `casita.rs` as of the owner's 19:06:58 UTC edit, which includes the
  19:00:18 trust fix. The owner edited only the test module of `casita.rs`
  after the run (19:07:28 onward), so the recorder cannot read the file as it
  ran. The fix hunk shown in the owner's session log is recorded under Run 32.
  The test is `tests/integration_build.rs:1083-1169` as of the CLI owner's
  19:06:29 UTC edit; the recorder read it after the run and it has not changed
  since (same file snapshot).
- Command: as Run 32, started 19:07:05 UTC. Its output starts with the
  identity: HEAD `7ec5177718a6950297e04eb4eb957a10b02e23ce`, dirty tree, the
  same status lines as Run 32.
- Exit code: 0; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 1 passed, 13 filtered out:
  `casita_explicit_trust_policy_revokes_local_signer_across_fresh_processes`.
  Each step runs in a new `mantle` process under `casita`. With a policy file
  that lists only another key, a build signed by the local key fails with
  `casita-signer-untrusted` in the JSON report, and the file keeps its bytes.
  With the local key listed, the build succeeds and `store info` shows one
  signature. With the key removed again, `store info` fails with
  `casita-signer-untrusted`, and the export bytes and policy file are
  unchanged. With the key restored, `store info` shows the same signature.
- Coverage: Run 24 is the failing-before counterpart. T2.14 is partial. Its
  "local signer listed" positive, "local signer unlisted" negative, and "local
  signer removed" fresh-process lookup now pass. Still missing: the local build
  without a policy file in a fresh process; malformed, empty, oversized, and
  over-limit policy files; a build signed by a `--signing-key` outside the
  trust set; a substituted output signed only by a cache key; and, after key or
  file removal, `store verify` and GC planning as well as lookup. T2.3 stays
  open with T2.14.

## Run 35: Casita adapter tests after the trust fix, passed

- Reported by: adapter owner, in a message to the parent (artifact://4585). Not
  rerun by the recorder. The wall time comes from the async result of `bg_780`.
- Source: `casita.rs#E55B`, the owner's 19:10:37 UTC edit. The owner's next
  edit (19:15:00 UTC) only inserted 21 test lines after line 1011. The recorder
  read the tests as they ran by removing those lines from the next snapshot
  (`#D7D6`), and the lines that the 19:10:37 edit result printed match that
  reconstruction.
- Command: as Run 34, started 19:10:43 UTC. The output starts with the
  identity: HEAD `7ec5177718a6950297e04eb4eb957a10b02e23ce`, dirty tree, the
  same status lines as Run 32. The CLI owner's 19:10:52 UTC edit to
  `overlay.rs` fell inside this run; it changed only an overlay test.
- Exit code: 0; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 4 passed, 389 filtered out:
  - `invalid_trust_policy_cannot_open_casita_repository`: a policy file that is
    empty, holds an unparsable key, holds 65 keys, or holds 65,537 bytes, and a
    dangling symlink in its place, each make `StoreHandle::open` under `casita`
    fail with `casita-trust-policy-invalid`. The file or link keeps its bytes,
    and no `casita/` repository is created.
  - `local_signer_without_policy_survives_fresh_process`: with a `signing-key`
    and no policy file, a PathInfo signed with that key is admitted through the
    store handle, and a child process (a new OS process running the same test
    binary) opens the store and reads it back with complete castore content. No
    policy file appears in either process.
  - `store_sign_rejects_second_client_repoint_between_read_and_update`: a
    second store handle on the same repository publishes a different signed
    envelope for the root after `store sign` read it. `store sign` fails with
    `casita-root-conflict`, and the root keeps the other client's target.
  - `signed_roots_survive_export_removal_and_reopen_with_conflicts_and_guard`:
    the Run 14 test, extended. Children are new OS processes. A
    content-addressed output and a signed output without a content address each
    verify in a child after the export is deleted; the child compares the
    stored `pathinfo.json` bytes with a fresh encoding of the decoded PathInfo
    and measures the NAR again. A batch whose second PathInfo carries signed
    but wrong NAR facts fails with `casita-nar-mismatch`; the first path's
    scratch copy stays in session castore, and neither path resolves in the
    parent or in a child. `store sign` with a second key replaces the envelope:
    `content` keeps its bytes, `pathinfo.json` changes, a child verifies the
    new signature, and no published root targets the old envelope. A second
    handle replaces the envelope between `store repair-final-nar` inspection
    and execution, so execution fails with `casita-root-conflict` and the root
    keeps the other target. Another Casita client then publishes altered
    envelopes one at a time: swapped `content`, `pathinfo.json` with an
    unsigned fact change, non-canonical JSON, a `pathinfo.json` over 1 MiB, a
    validly signed PathInfo whose NAR facts do not match `content`, and another
    output's PathInfo under this root name. A child rejects each with
    `casita-envelope-invalid`, `casita-signer-untrusted`,
    `casita-envelope-invalid: PathInfo is not canonical JSON`,
    `casita-envelope-invalid: PathInfo size`, `casita-nar-mismatch` (also on
    repair inspection, with the root unchanged), and
    `casita-envelope-invalid: root`. The Run 14 assertions for fences,
    retention, pressure collection, and a removed root still pass.
- Coverage: see Run 38, which runs these tests at the current source.

  `casita_gc_executes_signed_output_plan_and_preserves_retained_nar_facts`.
  Under `StoreMutationGuard`, that test admits two signed content-addressed
  outputs and registers one as a retained build root. The dry-run plan lists
  only the other output and counts one retained root. Executing that plan
  completes, reports a `CasitaCollection` operation and the same candidates,
  and leaves no fence pending. Afterward the retained output's PathInfo is
  unchanged, its NAR recalculated from castore has the recorded size and
  SHA-256, and its content address is `CAHash::Nar` of that SHA-256. The
  collected output no longer resolves, one verified GC root remains (the
  retained output), and the collected envelope can no longer be opened.
- Coverage: T3.3 is checked from this run. Every listed fixture passes in this
  one run, at one source snapshot: clean execution (the new test), stale
  candidate target
  (`casita_gc_repointed_verified_output_rejects_old_plan_before_fencing`,
  `casita_plan_identity_binds_exact_root_targets`), newly retained candidate
  (`casita_gc_rejects_newly_retained_root_then_collects_only_unretained`),
  interruption before and after root removal
  (`casita_recovery_before_first_root_removal_preserves_candidate`,
  `casita_recovery_replays_real_partial_root_removal_without_new_deletions`,
  `casita_output_fence_recovery_preserves_unremoved_and_cleans_only_removed_metadata`,
  and
  `casita_fence_recovers_partial_progress_without_marking_other_roots_removed`,
  as built crash state per Run 18), busy collector
  (`casita_gc_busy_collector_keeps_reclaim_incomplete_until_recovery`, on
  castore roots per Run 18), and equal decisions with `snix`
  (`casita_gc_matches_snix_candidates_for_identical_published_outputs_and_retention`).
  The Rust-retention payload clause applies only once `rust-unit-cache` is
  declared, and it is not. T3.6 is unchanged: it needs the `store gc` CLI
  positive with a retained output.

## Run 37: Casita archive import rejections with an unsigned record, passed

- Reported by: CLI owner, with the full output in two messages. Not rerun by
  the recorder. The output below is copied from artifact://4652; the wall time
  comes from the async result of `bg_787`.
- Source: `tests/store_archive_cli.rs#D110`, the CLI owner's last edit
  (19:03:11 UTC). The owner read the file with that snapshot until 19:21:11
  UTC, and the recorder read `tests/store_archive_cli.rs:133-317` after the run
  with the same snapshot. The library compiled `casita.rs#D7D6`.
- Command (mantle repository root), started 19:20:20 UTC. It prints the git
  identity itself, right before the test:
  `git rev-parse HEAD && git status --short --branch && nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/brittonr/.cargo-target cargo test --locked --offline --config .cargo/vendor-config.toml -p mantle --test store_archive_cli casita_archive_import_rejects_unprovisioned_keys_and_unnamed_signer_without_mutation -- --nocapture`.
  The identity lines equal Run 32's.
- Exit code: 0; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 1 passed, 9 filtered out:
  `casita_archive_import_rejects_unprovisioned_keys_and_unnamed_signer_without_mutation`.
  A `snix` source exports a signed archive and, with `--trust-unsigned`, an
  archive holding one unsigned PathInfo. Each case imports into a new `casita`
  destination that `store list` opened first. The cases are: missing policy
  (`casita-trust-policy-missing`), a policy key other than the requested one
  and a policy key with the same name but other bytes
  (`casita-import-key-unauthorized`), a requested key that did not sign the
  archive (`untrusted-signature`), and the unsigned archive imported with the
  policy and `--trusted-public-keys` naming the source signer
  (`casita-signer-untrusted`). After each failure, the destination's
  Mantle-owned files (everything outside `casita/` except
  `store-mutation.lock`) keep their names and BLAKE3 digests, the output
  directory is unchanged, the policy file keeps its bytes or stays absent,
  every source state file keeps its bytes, and `store list --json` shows no
  path.
- Coverage: T3.5, the unsigned record in a signed import, now has a CLI fixture
  with the source-state and policy-file assertions (see Run 39 for the same
  test after the key-comparison change).

## Run 38: Casita adapter tests with same-handle policy changes, passed

- Reported by: adapter owner (artifact://4663). Not rerun by the recorder. The
  wall time comes from the async result of `bg_788`.
- Source: `casita.rs#6C31`, the owner's 19:22:23 UTC edit. That edit makes the
  archive preflight compare `VerifyingKey` values directly instead of their
  string forms, and it changes a doc comment in `handle.rs`. The test module is
  Run 35's plus the 21 lines inserted at 19:15:00 UTC (`casita.rs:1012-1032`).
  No agent edited a source file during the run, and the recorder read the tests
  after the run with the same snapshot.
- Command: as Run 34, started 19:22:33 UTC. The identity lines equal Run 32's.
- Exit code: 0; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 4 passed, 389 filtered out; the tests of Run 35. New in
  `signed_roots_survive_export_removal_and_reopen_with_conflicts_and_guard`
  (`casita.rs:1005-1032`): after the handle `reopened` opens, the policy file
  is rewritten to list fixture keys 1 and 2, and an output signed only by key 2
  is admitted through that handle. The file is then rewritten to list only key
  1. Through the same handle, lookup, `store_verify`, and guarded GC planning
  each fail with `casita-signer-untrusted`, and the output root keeps its
  target. With key 2 listed again, the next lookup through the same handle
  returns the output.
- Coverage:
  - T2.13 is checked from this run. Positive: a content-addressed output and
    one without a content address each verify in a new process, and the stored
    `pathinfo.json` equals a fresh encoding of the decoded PathInfo. Negative:
    NAR mismatch under a valid signature, `pathinfo.json` with an unsigned fact
    change, `pathinfo.json` over 1 MiB, non-canonical JSON that still decodes,
    swapped `content`, another output's PathInfo under this root name, and a
    scratch copy after a failed batch publication; each is rejected in a new
    process, as Run 35 describes.
  - T2.10: every fixture that it lists passes in this run. Positive:
    `store sign` keeps `content`, a new process verifies the new signature, and
    no published root targets the old envelope. Negative: a second handle
    repoints the root during `store sign`
    (`store_sign_rejects_second_client_repoint_between_read_and_update`) and
    during `store repair-final-nar`; each fails with `casita-root-conflict` and
    leaves the other target. A repair inspection on an envelope whose signed
    facts do not match `content` fails with `casita-nar-mismatch`, and the root
    keeps its target. The recorder checked T2.10 from this run and then
    unchecked it: the parent's Decision B makes `store repair-final-nar`
    unsupported under `casita` until a durable repair fence exists, and T2.5
    and T2.10 must not be marked complete; their repair fixtures will change
    with the contract.
  - T2.14: partial. New: invalid policy files fail the open
    (`invalid_trust_policy_cannot_open_casita_repository`); a PathInfo signed
    with the local key and no policy file verifies in a new process with no
    policy file created (library level); and same-handle removal and
    re-addition of a key. Still missing: see the task status below.
  - T3.4: the Run 14 retention, pressure-collection, and removed-root
    assertions pass again at this source.

## Run 40: sandboxed Casita build with a generated signer and no policy file, one-off smoke, passed

- Reported by: CLI owner, with the command and output in a message. Not rerun
  by the recorder. The JSON output is artifact://4686 and the identity capture
  is artifact://4684; the wall time comes from the async result of `bg_792`.
  This is a one-off script, not a permanent fixture.
- Identity: captured at 19:27:52 UTC, eight seconds before the smoke, with
  `git rev-parse HEAD && git status --short --branch && b3sum /home/brittonr/.cargo-target/debug/mantle`:
  HEAD `7ec5177718a6950297e04eb4eb957a10b02e23ce`, the same status lines as Run
  32, and binary BLAKE3
  `d2f771dbf3b4942b44ba20f74756e089f6e4c0dbd35f28dc19fccbe527e2a887`. The CLI
  owner's `bg_791` test run (started 19:26:11 UTC) last built that binary; no
  agent edited a source file between 19:22:24 and the smoke, so it contains the
  sources of Run 38.
- Command (mantle repository root), started 19:28:00 UTC. It runs a Python
  script (3,551 bytes) inside the dev shell:
  `nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env -u CARGO_HOME SNIX_BUILD_SANDBOX_SHELL=/bin/sh python3 -c <script>`.
  The script, verbatim:

```text
import base64, json, os, pathlib, subprocess, tempfile
BINARY = "/home/brittonr/.cargo-target/debug/mantle"
REPO = pathlib.Path("/home/brittonr/git/OnixResearch/mantle")
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="mantle-casita-auto-key-sandbox-") as scratch:
    root = pathlib.Path(scratch)
    state, store = root / "state", root / "store"
    state.mkdir(); store.mkdir()
    signer, policy = state / "signing-key", state / "casita-trusted-public-keys"
    assert not signer.exists() and not policy.exists()
    env = dict(os.environ)
    env.pop("CRUNCH_CONFIG_DIR", None)
    def run(*action):
        command = [BINARY, "--store-backend", "casita", "--state-dir", str(state), "--store", str(store), *map(str, action)]
        result = subprocess.run(command, cwd=REPO, env=env, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120)
        if result.returncode:
            raise AssertionError(f"{action} exited {result.returncode}: stderr={result.stderr[:6000]} stdout={result.stdout[:6000]}")
        return result.stdout
    build = ("--json", "build", "--no-substitute", "--import-path", REPO / "lib", REPO / "examples/hello.ncl")
    first = json.loads(run(*build))
    assert first["counts"]["built_total"] == 1 and first["counts"]["cached_total"] == 0 and first["outcomes"][0]["cached"] is False, first
    assert signer.is_file() and not policy.exists(), "build must generate signer without trust policy"
    secret_bytes = signer.read_bytes()
    signer_name, encoded = secret_bytes.decode().strip().split(":", 1)
    raw = base64.b64decode(encoded)
    assert len(raw) == 64, "generated Ed25519 keypair expected"
    public = signer_name + ":" + base64.b64encode(raw[32:]).decode()
    path = pathlib.Path(first["outcomes"][0]["outputs"][0]["path"])
    assert path.parent == store and path.read_bytes() == b"Hello, mantle!\n", path
    original_info = json.loads(run("--json", "store", "info", path.name))["paths"][0]
    assert len(original_info["signatures"]) == 1 and original_info["signatures"][0].startswith(signer_name + ":")
    path.unlink()
    assert not path.exists()
    second = json.loads(run(*build))
    assert second["counts"]["built_total"] == 0 and second["counts"]["cached_total"] == 1 and second["outcomes"][0]["cached"] is True, second
    restored = pathlib.Path(second["outcomes"][0]["outputs"][0]["path"])
    assert restored == path and restored.read_bytes() == b"Hello, mantle!\n", restored
    restored_info = json.loads(run("--json", "store", "info", path.name))["paths"][0]
    for field in ("store_path", "nar_sha256", "nar_size", "signatures"):
        assert restored_info[field] == original_info[field], field
    verified = run("store", "verify", "--trusted-public-keys", public, path.name).strip()
    assert "trusted_signatures=1/1" in verified, verified
    assert signer.read_bytes() == secret_bytes and not policy.exists(), "fresh process changed local signer/policy"
    for snix_file in ("pathinfo.redb", "directories.redb", "blobs"):
        assert not (state / snix_file).exists(), snix_file
    print(json.dumps({"status":"PASS", "scenario":"casita-sandbox-local-generated-signer-no-policy", "first_counts":first["counts"], "second_counts":second["counts"], "store_path":restored_info["store_path"], "nar_sha256":restored_info["nar_sha256"], "nar_size":restored_info["nar_size"], "signatures":restored_info["signatures"], "local_signer_generated":True, "local_signer_unchanged":True, "policy_absent":True, "store_verify_explicit_derived_public_key":verified, "no_snix_state":True}, sort_keys=True))
```
- Exit code: 0; the async result has no non-zero exit footer.
- Scenario: under `casita`, a new state directory with no `signing-key` and no
  policy file, and `CRUNCH_CONFIG_DIR` unset. `mantle build --no-substitute`
  builds `examples/hello.ncl` in the sandbox (built 1, cached 0); Mantle
  generates `signing-key` and creates no policy file. After the export is
  deleted, a second process reuses the output (built 0, cached 1) and restores
  `Hello, mantle!\n` at the same path; the store path, NAR SHA-256, NAR size,
  and signatures equal the first process's.
  `store verify --trusted-public-keys <public half of the generated key>`
  reports `trusted_signatures=1/1`. The signing key keeps its bytes, no policy
  file appears, and no `pathinfo.redb`, `directories.redb`, or `blobs` exists
  in the state directory.
- Note: the script passes the public key to `store verify` explicitly. Under
  `casita` without `--signing-key`, `store verify` reports trusted signatures
  only for configured or explicit keys and does not load or generate the local
  key (`src/store_cmd.rs:1128-1140`); reads still verify under the adapter's
  trust set.
- Coverage: T4.3 gains a fifth fresh-process observation, after Runs 21, 22,
  30, and 31; the conformance rail is still missing. T2.14's local build
  without a policy file now has a CLI observation beside the library fixture of
  Run 38. No task is checked.

Full output, verbatim from artifact://4686 (786 bytes, SHA-256
`afe70eb0137dff213fe7543e29a759fa7bf4a77385ecb8e9ab588bc992ffa633`), followed by the
wall-time footer from the async result of `bg_792`:

```text
{"first_counts": {"built_total": 1, "cached_total": 0, "failed_total": 0, "succeeded_total": 1}, "local_signer_generated": true, "local_signer_unchanged": true, "nar_sha256": "86b90edc64f470240656da9a80f655d3b57c19be6b9ba41e03e4596732e1dd01", "nar_size": 128, "no_snix_state": true, "policy_absent": true, "scenario": "casita-sandbox-local-generated-signer-no-policy", "second_counts": {"built_total": 0, "cached_total": 1, "failed_total": 0, "succeeded_total": 1}, "signatures": ["crunch-britton-desktop-1:6sTttgxUD+938MC/6GTAqRtydTFu+d2SqnUWPsTtA33Gus+GQEAvbF9fSCcysulJSJyAzF/u51/r5z+TAlhYCw=="], "status": "PASS", "store_path": "z2y3k7267f9kbpfyw9zw0d1adjcn6z62-hello", "store_verify_explicit_derived_public_key": "OK z2y3k7267f9kbpfyw9zw0d1adjcn6z62-hello  trusted_signatures=1/1"}
```

  UTC, and the recorder read both fixtures after the runs with the same
  snapshots.
- Command (mantle repository root), started 19:51:34 UTC. It prints the git
  identity itself, right before the test:
  `git rev-parse HEAD && git status --short --branch && nix develop --offline --command cargo test -q -p crunch-store --lib repair::tests::casita_repair_library_entrypoints_reject_without_publishing -- --exact --nocapture`.
  The identity lines equal Run 32's.
- Exit code: 0; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixture:
  `repair::tests::casita_repair_library_entrypoints_reject_without_publishing`:
  a stale signed PathInfo is admitted to a Snix store, and its repair
  inspection on that store succeeds. A Casita store is then opened in another
  state directory, where the path is absent. `inspect_final_nar_repair` on the
  Casita store fails with `casita-repair-final-nar-unsupported`, and so does
  `execute_final_nar_repair` given the Snix inspection. Afterward the path is
  still absent from the Casita store, and the Snix PathInfo is unchanged. Both
  library functions return the blocker as their first statement
  (`repair.rs:142-144` and `repair.rs:200-202`).
- Coverage: see Run 46.

## Run 47: Casita adapter tests at the current source after the repair cut, passed

- Reported by: lint owner (`mantle-tigerstyle-fixes`, artifact://4878). Not
  rerun by the recorder. The command ran in the foreground; the wall time comes
  from the tool result.
- Source: `crates/crunch-store/src/casita.rs#822F`. Since Run 38 the lint owner
  fixed lint findings and removed the repair steps from the tests (19:52:57
  UTC), ran rustfmt (19:54:01), renamed a variable in `prepare_output_envelope`
  (20:00:04), added `gc_inventory_reads_more_roots_than_one_atomic_mutation`
  (20:00:35), and ran rustfmt again (20:00:42). The owner's reads at 20:01:55,
  20:03:12, 20:04:49, and 20:05:55 UTC all show `#822F`, no agent edited a
  source file between 20:00:43 and the end of the run (20:07:14), and the
  recorder read the tests after the run with the same snapshot.
- Command (mantle repository root), started 20:06:34 UTC. It prints the git
  identity itself, right before the test:
  `git rev-parse HEAD && git status --short --branch && nix develop --offline --no-write-lock-file --command env NIX_CONFIG='substituters =' cargo test -p crunch-store --lib casita::tests:: -- --test-threads=1`.
  HEAD is `7ec5177718a6950297e04eb4eb957a10b02e23ce`; the status lines equal
  Run 32's plus ` M crates/crunch-store/tests/authority_source_policy.rs`,
  which the lint owner edited at 19:53:45 UTC.
- Exit code: 0; the output has no non-zero exit footer.
- Fixtures run: 5 passed, 391 filtered out:
  - The four tests of Run 38.
    `signed_roots_survive_export_removal_and_reopen_with_conflicts_and_guard`
    no longer runs `store repair-final-nar`: the lint owner removed the
    inspection that reported `Current`, the repair race through a second
    handle, and the parent-side `casita-nar-mismatch` repair inspection. Every
    child-process rejection remains (`casita.rs:1511-1570`), including
    `casita-nar-mismatch` for a validly signed PathInfo whose NAR facts do not
    match `content`. The later steps now use the PathInfo that `store sign`
    produced, which carries both fixture signatures.
  - `gc_inventory_reads_more_roots_than_one_atomic_mutation`: 1,025 signed
    content-addressed outputs are published by two `put_batch_atomic` calls,
    the first with exactly `max_root_changes` records (1,024 at the pinned
    defaults) and the second with one. `verified_gc_roots` then returns all
    1,025 roots, each read and verified, including the last one.
- Coverage:
  - T2.10: the `store sign` positive (same `content`, changed `pathinfo.json`,
    no published root targeting the old envelope, a child process that verifies
    the new signature) and the `store sign` race (`casita-root-conflict`, the
    other handle's target kept) pass at the current source. With Runs 44 to 46,
    every T2.10 item has a passing fixture, but two listed assertions are only
    partly asserted, so T2.10 stays open. First, the library refusal is
    required to change nothing, and Run 44 checks only that no PathInfo for the
    path appears in the Casita store and that the Snix PathInfo is unchanged;
    neither state directory is compared. Second, the CLI refusal is required
    not to modify the state directory as a dry run and with `--execute`, and
    Run 45 checks an existing state directory only with `--execute`; the dry
    run runs only against a missing directory.
  - T2.5 is checked from this run, Runs 44 to 46, and the code review that
    follows.
  - T2.13 and T3.4: their assertions pass again at `#822F`.
  - T2.11: partial. A library batch of exactly 1,024 records publishes, and
    every envelope verifies through the GC inventory. Missing: a Nario v2
    import, a check that the batch commits in one Casita revision, and the
    negatives.

## Code review for T2.5

Not a test run. The recorder read the current source after Run 47, as the
parent required before T2.5 could be checked.

- Conditional `store sign` replacement. `store_sign`
  (`crates/crunch-store/src/query.rs:265-330`, `#371A`) lists PathInfo through
  the store and puts each updated PathInfo back. Under `casita`,
  `CasitaStore::list` (`casita.rs:839-860`, `#822F`) reads every output root
  through `verified_output_roots` with observation recording on, and each
  verified read records the root target it read (`casita.rs:295-299`).
  `CasitaStore::put` (`casita.rs:826-829`) allows replacement;
  `put_batch_atomic` (`casita.rs:831-837`) does not. `publish_pathinfos`
  (`casita.rs:697-808`) accepts an identical envelope without a write.
  Otherwise it rejects a replacement with `casita-root-conflict` unless the
  current root target equals the recorded one (`casita.rs:740-753`). It stages
  the new envelope unrooted through `prepare_output_envelope`, which repeats
  the admission checks: a signature from the trust set and content-address
  identity (`casita.rs:580-587`), NAR facts measured against the signed
  PathInfo, export, re-ingest to the same node, and a second NAR measurement
  (`casita.rs:589-640`). It then commits `RootChange::Set` with the recorded
  target as the expectation, and a root mismatch commits nothing and fails with
  `casita-root-conflict` (`casita.rs:760-767`, `790-806`). The content comes
  from the unchanged node, so the new envelope keeps the same `content`; Run 47
  compares the bytes.
- Profile. `crates/crunch-store/src/backend.rs:36-78` (`#BFE0`) gives `snix` a
  core list with `store-repair-final-nar` and `casita` one without it.
- CLI. `preflight_casita_cli_backend` (`src/main.rs:3303-3311`, `#1707`)
  rejects `store repair-final-nar` under `casita` with
  `casita-repair-final-nar-unsupported`, with or without `--execute`.
  `run_for_platform` calls it at line 3358, before `apply_state_dir_override`,
  `build_run_context`, and dispatch (lines 3368-3383).
  `src/store_cmd.rs:224-231` (`#0A2A`) repeats the check before the mutation
  guard and the store open.
- Library. `inspect_final_nar_repair` and `execute_final_nar_repair` return the
  blocker as their first statement (`crates/crunch-store/src/repair.rs:142-144`
  and `200-202`, `#C3CA`). Every guard matches only `StoreBackend::Casita`, so
  `snix` repair takes the path it took before.
- Evidence currency. No agent edited `repair.rs`, `backend.rs`, `src/main.rs`,
  `src/store_cmd.rs`, or `tests/integration.rs` after the repair owner's last
  edit (19:47:14 UTC), and no later rustfmt run included them, so Runs 44 to 46
  exercised the current guard code and fixtures. They predate the lint changes
  to `casita.rs`, `gc.rs`, `roots.rs`, `overlay.rs`, `handle.rs`, and
  `nario.rs`; Run 47 covers those changes only for the Casita adapter tests.
